## 2026-11-11 — Restore Windows Gemini verification and model loading (1.0.289)

Reproduced the installed Windows failure with a fake key: FileNotFoundError occurred constructing SSLContext before any Gemini request. Working source Python’s ssl.py is customized to load codex-rejected-cas.pem beside the module; the frozen engine omitted that companion certificate-policy file. An isolated frozen diagnostic copy reproduced the traceback, and adding the exact runtime policy file restored a normal Google invalid-key response. Packaging now discovers and includes the optional policy file, preserving TLS verification and runtime policy. Both engines’ existing doctor command now initializes a verified, hostname-checking SSL context and reports sslReady; Windows engine packaging rejects a failing frozen health check. PyInstaller informational stderr is forwarded safely under PowerShell while actual exit failures remain fatal.

Built/installed 1.0.289 (Mac build 289, Windows ARM64 package 1.0.289.0). A live check using the existing Windows Credential Manager key, kept in process memory and passed only through the child environment, succeeds: keyValid=true, 36 models, Paid Plan. The live test used a temporary credential-free config, preserved user settings, and emitted only validity/plan/count. This verifies the actual installed engine’s key/catalog path; hands-on native model-picker interaction was not separately exercised. Packaged native context-menu smoke and 48 Windows-host source tests pass. Shared menu checks pass hierarchy/order and 49 shortcuts; 42 focused source menu/context tests pass. Twenty-seven temporary-data tests pass against the installed Mac engine, and its doctor reports sslReady=true.

Mac app, Share and Finder extensions verify at 1.0.289/build 289 with stable team LLVWNNUV26 and signing-continuity/registration checks; Spotlight lists only /Applications/VidiStudio.app. Windows final verification confirms one current package and Start entry, Status Ok, no stale app outputs and 48 installed Help files matching current sources. The normal Windows app is reopened. Canonical Help and generated Windows Help include recovery guidance; installed Mac Help verifies 14 pages, registration, links, anchors and search index. Native Help Viewer interaction was not rerun. Website Gemini API guidance is updated; TypeScript/build pass. Live website publication remains pending the earlier explicit authorization request; no deployment or new upload credential request occurred.

## 2026-11-11 — Contextual segment actions and grouped transport (1.0.280 / build 280)

On Mac and Windows, timed-section Edit mode adds Select All/Deselect All followed by individual segment actions to Edit. Exactly one visible selected row is required for individual edit, video navigation, transcript re-analysis, and description synthesis/rewrite/import/audio deletion. Delete Selected Segments is the exception and accepts any nonempty selection. AD Transcript follows the selected row kind: transcript rows omit narration operations; audio description rows omit transcript re-analysis. Summary, AD Overview and viewing mode omit these Edit entries. Right-click keeps individual-row actions in viewing and Edit modes and targets its clicked row. Both row menus use the shared guarded dispatcher; Windows deletion preserves an unrelated row of the other kind even when IDs match.

Playback > Segment Narration groups selected-description playback separately from video controls. Its one primary action is Play Narration / Pause Narration / Resume Narration. A separate Stop Narration is present only while that row has active or paused narration. The row buttons and right-click menu share these states and handlers. The Playback entries require Edit mode in Descriptions or AD Transcript and exactly one selected audio description. Starting requires usable synchronized saved audio, matching the actual row button; missing/outdated audio disables Play. Context menus separate editing, video navigation, narration transport, creation/import, and deletion with dividers; audio-only and whole-segment deletion are together in the last group. More Actions separates its destructive group too. Video menus and the timeline transport share Play Video/Pause Video and conditional Stop Video: hidden before playback and after Stop/completion, retained while paused. No new keyboard shortcuts.

Menu stability/performance: Mac row popup items are reused when titles, images, availability and group boundaries are unchanged, while callbacks still refresh. An active-preview task replaces the idle 200 ms publisher. Rendering row availability no longer rebuilds the visible row list or checks audio for unrelated actions; action dispatch still validates the current visible target. Playback observers publish only real playing/buffering changes. Callback properties are excluded from Observation and unchanged project snapshots are not republished to command menus. Windows retains unchanged title/icon instances and updates playback menus only when transport state changes. The Mac icon decorator no longer decorates at menu-opening/tracking time or in response to its own item notifications; decoration is coalesced while menus are closed. AppKit tracking notifications identify the entire main menu bar, so decorating it during opening affected every submenu. The native regression uses that root object and confirms zero decoration at opening or during tracking, coalescing 100 closed-menu notifications and ignoring 100 open-menu notifications without recursion.

Validation: 86 Mac focused tests pass, covering compiled production Swift/C# tab/selection/prerequisite policies, native popup item reuse, latest callback dispatch, real Swift Observation invalidation, native AppKit notification bursts, transport session state, editing/deletion/re-analysis and menu parity. Both combined desktop menu checkers pass hierarchy/order and 49 translated shortcuts. Windows C# semantic Compile with WinUI/SDK references and temporary generated-XAML field stand-ins passes with 0 errors and 7 existing host/tooling warnings. Native Windows install/UI is blocked: the current prlctl start attempt again reports that Pro or Business Edition is required. Windows source/package versions are 1.0.280 / 1.0.280.0; Windows installation is not verified.

Installed signed /Applications/VidiStudio.app using script/build_and_run.sh --verify, with app, Share and Finder extensions all at 1.0.280/build 280 and stable Apple Development team LLVWNNUV26. Native QA verified zero/one/multiple selection behavior, opening the single transcript editor, the two-row deletion confirmation (cancelled), description-specific Edit actions, AD Transcript transcript selection, grouped narration Play/Pause/Resume/Stop, shared right-click paused state and visible context dividers. ## 2026-11-11 — Prevent same-language copies (1.0.279 / build 279)

## 2026-10-31 — Hide API-key deletion when empty (1.0.193 / build 193)

Mac and Windows now hide the Delete Gemini API key control for empty or whitespace-only input, rather than leaving a disabled trash icon visible. It appears only when a key is present, updates as the field changes, and disappears after deletion. Both delete actions reject an empty key before confirmation or removal; Windows rechecks after confirmation, and the Mac model rejects empty/busy deletion requests. The Mac secure field uses observable local input state so visibility responds immediately while credentials remain stored only in Keychain.

## 2026-10-30 — Fix the reported Summary title and blank Help TOC (1.0.177 / 1.0.177.0)

The user's screenshots exposed two gaps in the earlier validation. Their actual summary starts with **five U+FEFF byte-order marks** left inside the JSON string by older Windows saves. The leading marks stop the first `#` heading from matching even though later headings render normally. Summary reading and editing now remove these leading encoding marks in memory, normalize line breaks, and keep the existing project untouched on open. The shared Windows/Mac save helper strips them on a future explicit save and keeps the origin metadata. The Mac reader uses the same leading-mark/newline cleanup. The Windows document title now has a clearer 30-point heading hierarchy and spacing. Removed the tooltip on the entire summary reading surface that covered the title on hover; double-click editing and the labeled Edit button remain available.

The Help TOC's node data was present, but its template bound `Title`/`Icon` directly even though `RootNodes` templates receive a `TreeViewNode`. The corrected paths are `Content.Title` and `Content.Icon`, including the automation name. This follows the node-template model in [Microsoft's TreeView documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/develop/ui/controls/tree-view). TOC text now renders in both themes. Help's initial size now accounts for monitor DPI and is bounded to the work area, and only the current chapter starts expanded so the chapter list remains easy to scan.

**Correction to 1.0.176's Help evidence:** the earlier smoke assertion checked the HelpNode title values, not the visible TextBlocks, so it could pass while the TOC was blank. The new smoke check walks the actual rendered template labels, requires multiple visible chapter/topic names and the first six chapter titles at the test display scaling, and checks light/dark appearance. Native test PNGs now composite their transparent XAML/Mica content onto the matching theme background so image viewers show the actual text contrast instead of transparent white rows or black text on transparent black.

Validation: the 63-test focused import/summary/Markdown/menu/Help suite passes; matching Mac source-level Summary/menu tests pass 18 tests using Windows Python (not a Mac runtime test). Full **ARM64 and x64 native UI suites pass with the bundled engine and a read-only copy of the exact 2,611-character summary from the screenshot**, including all five leading marks, the corrected title/readback/save behavior, visible Help TOC labels, DPI sizing, search/navigation/themes, and the broader import/combine workflow. Visual artifacts: `build/ui-test-x64/summary-presentation.png`, `help-toc-dark.png`, and `help-toc-light.png`; logs: `build/summary-177-ui.log`, `build/summary-177-ui-x64.log`, `build/summary-177-regressions.log`. The original user's project.json remains byte-for-byte unchanged, verified against its pre-test SHA-256. ARM64 Release packaging and MSIX inspection pass; x64 native smoke compilation/run passes. Shared menu checks, 53-function export parity, XAML references, and strict version synchronization pass.

## 2026-10-30 - Render edited summaries as Markdown (1.0.175 / 1.0.175.0)

Editing a summary and saving left the reading view showing the raw Markdown. Two defects combined. WinUI `TextBox.Text` exposes line breaks as lone `\r`, while `BuildMarkdownView` normalized only `\r\n`; the whole edited summary was therefore treated as one line, often matching the heading pattern and rendering `#`, `**`, and `-` literally. Separately, `SaveSummaryChangesAsync` compared the editor text with `Project.Summary` **after** `SaveSummaryAsync` had already assigned that property, so the post-save `RenderMarkdown` was skipped whenever the save succeeded (the comparison was false).

Fixes: normalize `\r\n` and lone `\r` to `\n` in `BuildMarkdownView`; normalize the editor text before saving and before the unsaved-changes comparison; always refresh `SummaryText` and re-render the reading view after a save, independent of the pre-save comparison. The same normalization now feeds the Markdown toolbar commands, Enter list continuation, and the Preview dialog, so heading and list formatting also works on text typed in the editor. Added native smoke coverage that renders `# Heading\r\rA **bold** line.\r\r- Item`, requires three separate blocks, then edits the summary, saves it, and requires the reading view to show a heading, bold run, and list item with no raw `**` markers; the original summary is restored afterward.

Validation: the full ARM64 native UI smoke suite passes with the new regression checks. ARM64 Debug/Release with `-Package` and x64 Debug builds pass. Version synchronization reports Windows 1.0.175 / package 1.0.175.0 and macOS 1.0.175 / build 175 in sync. Removed the 1.0.174 registration and install folder, unpacked `Vidi Studio Windows_1.0.175.0_arm64.msix` into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\VidiStudio\app-1.0.175.0`, registered it, and confirmed exactly one `Get-AppxPackage` entry at 1.0.175.0. The installed app launched responsively (`App constructed · version 1.0.175.0` through `Launch completed`). Not run: macOS build/launch, live Gemini, Windows 10, or high-contrast runtime validation.

## 2026-10-29 - Repair the Help book build and Windows Help content (1.0.174 / 1.0.174.0)

The latest Help commit (`3f8cede`, "Fixed help documentation on windows") broke the Windows build and the Help window itself. `SettingsDialog.Information.cs` read `menuOwner?.Root.ActualTheme`, but the generated `Root` field is private to `MainWindow`, so the build failed with CS0122. Changing `HelpWindow.xaml` also left stale generated fields (`HomeButton`, `Home_Click`, `BrowserHelpButton`, `BrowserHelp_Click`) in `obj\...\Views\HelpWindow.g.cs`; a clean XAML rebuild is required after that change. The window's table of contents had been changed to put a `StackPanel` directly into each `TreeViewNode.Content` with no `ItemTemplate`. WinUI's TreeView then displayed every node through `TreeViewNode.ToString()`, so all 12 chapters and 92 topics appeared as `Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls.TreeViewNode`; UI Automation exposed the same type names, breaking screen-reader navigation. The commit also removed the old "Windows edition" note while leaving the Help prose full of macOS wording and duplicated shortcut notation.

Fixes: added `MainWindow.CurrentTheme` (internal `ElementTheme`) and used it from the Settings Help button, keeping the Help window theme in sync. Restored the TreeView `ItemTemplate` with title/icon bindings and `AutomationProperties.Name="{Binding Title}"`, set node `Content` back to `HelpNode`, restored `Toc_SelectionChanged` to read `HelpNode`, overrode `HelpNode.ToString()` to return its title, and removed the broken `NodeView` helper. Rewrote the Windows Help HTML to remove Mac-only shortcuts and terminology: shortcut tables now have a single Windows column, `Command-…` pairs and `(Mac)/(Windows)` qualifiers are gone, and the guide uses Ctrl/Alt, File > Settings, File Explorer, Recycle Bin, Windows Settings, Windows Credential Manager, and Narrator. Corrected the Markdown Undo/Redo shortcut to Ctrl-Z / Ctrl-Y (matching the in-app Markdown Help), removed the duplicated File Explorer instructions in Create your first accessible video and Share or open a video, fixed the narration wording and grammar (`Choose a Windows voice`), and updated the Settings Help copy that still referenced the Mac Help book, Mac menus, and Apple voices. `tests.test_help_window` now checks every manifest anchor, rejects Mac-only strings, and verifies the TOC template; the native smoke test now waits for the Help book to load, requires all chapters/topics in the TOC with readable titles, and opens/closes the Help window through the shared menu command.

Validation: ARM64 Debug with `-Rebuild`, ARM64 Release with `-Package`, and x64 Debug builds pass. `check_menu_parity.py` passes against the combined workspace (39 shortcuts); `check_xaml_references.py` passes; `tests.test_help_window` (4), import UI/XAML tests (23), and menu/parity tests (33) pass. The full ARM64 native UI smoke suite passes, including loading the installed Help book (12 chapters, 92 topics), the welcome page, readable TOC titles, and closing the Help window. Version synchronization reports Windows 1.0.174 / package 1.0.174.0 and macOS 1.0.174 / build 174 in sync. Installed the rebuilt package on this ARM64 host: removed the previous 1.0.170 registration and install folder, unpacked `Vidi Studio Windows_1.0.174.0_arm64.msix` with `makeappx` into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\VidiStudio\app-1.0.174.0`, registered that manifest, and confirmed exactly one `Get-AppxPackage` entry at 1.0.174.0. The app launched responsively, and UI Automation opened `Vidi Studio Help` from the Help menu and showed real chapter titles (`Welcome to VidiStudio`, `Create your first accessible video`, and so on) instead of `TreeViewNode`; every installed Help file hashes identically to the source. Not run: macOS build/launch, live Gemini, Windows 10, or high-contrast runtime validation.

## 2026-10-29 - Restore the Windows launch path (1.0.170 / 1.0.170.0)

The application could not launch because the last two commits shipped code that never compiled. `HEAD` (`6c1f250`, sorting and filtering) and its parent (`b1e24ab`, import system) left five build errors: `MainWindow.ImportMenus.cs` used `Func<Task>` without `using System`, `MainWindow.Imports.cs` assigned the read-only model `Status`, `MainWindow.Markdown.cs` set the unsupported `TextBlock.TextIndent`, `MainWindow.SummaryMarkdown.cs` used nonexistent `VirtualKey.D7`/`D8`/`D9`, and `MainWindow.BulkActions.cs` set `Segment.Speaker`, which the model did not have. `script/build-windows.ps1` stopped at compilation, so no current package existed. The only registered development package pointed at a stale 1.0.148 loose layout from 27 October, and running the loose `bin` executable directly fails with `REGDB_E_CLASSNOTREG (0x80040154)` because a packaged WinUI 3 app needs package identity.

Fixes: added `Segment.Speaker` as a nullable `speaker` JSON field (omitted when unset) so merged transcript rows keep engine/macOS diarization labels without writing `speaker: null` that the engine rejects; added the missing `using System`; routed the import completion message through `Model.ReportStatus`; replaced `TextIndent` with a left `Margin` for list and quote lines; mapped the markdown list shortcuts to `VirtualKey.Number7`/`Number8`/`Number9`. The markdown renderer now stores `StripInline` plain text for headings, task lists, numbered lists, bullets, and quotes, so summary readback highlighting and spoken text match the visible text instead of containing `**` markers. The native smoke test now expects the shared Project → Import group and verifies the new native Help book window (with a DEBUG-only `App.HelpWindowForSmokeTest` accessor) rather than the retired Settings page. Also fixed the `HashSet<string?>` nullability warning in `StudioProject.Load`.

Validation: `script/build-windows.ps1` passed for ARM64 Debug, ARM64 Release with `-Package`, and x64 Debug with no compile errors. `check_menu_parity.py` passed against the combined workspace (`C:\Mac\Home\Documents\GitHub\VidiStudio`) with 39 translated shortcuts; `tests.test_menu_parity` and `tests.test_parity` passed (23 tests), and 109 further tests passed across import UI/assets, Help window, Markdown commands, XAML references, project menus, and segment/transcript reanalysis. The full ARM64 native UI smoke suite passed, including themes, selection, summary highlighting, dialogs, narration, exports, and the new Help window. Version sync reports Windows 1.0.170 / package 1.0.170.0 and macOS 1.0.170 / build 170 in sync; the Mac pbxproj was updated with `sync_version.py --set 1.0.170 --build 170` but was not built here.

## 2026-10-27 - Prepare dialog windows before showing them (1.0.147 / 1.0.147.0)

Removed the oversized opening flash from the shared companion-dialog lifecycle. Previously the host activated a large initial window, then fitted it from the ContentDialog Opened event. The host now creates just one native window, loads and measures its tree while it remains genuinely hidden, fits the DPI-scaled client bounds and centers it, then activates it once at the final size. No temporary window, off-screen placement, opacity trick, or timed startup delay is used. A dedicated disposable layout controller replaces the Opened-time resize callbacks; later real content changes still resize that same window. The nested ContentDialog zoom/open-close transitions are removed, leaving native window animation only. Theme, owner-close, layout, Loaded/Opened/Closed handlers, and host Escape accelerators are detached on completion; canceling preparation never presents the window.

Native regression coverage observes creation, readiness, visibility, and window-size events, requiring a single fully laid-out hidden window before presentation and no visible startup resizing. Focused checks cover initial light/dark appearance, live light/dark/system switching, all dialog subclasses, genuine content growth in the existing window, cancellation before presentation, keyboard-accelerator cleanup, delete gating, and Primary/Secondary/Cancel results. The x64 test waits for the actual readiness event rather than assuming hidden preparation finishes within 350 ms.

Validation: focused ARM64 and x64-emulated native suites passed; the final full ARM64 UI smoke suite passed after rebuilding; ARM64 Release and x64 Debug builds passed using `script/build-windows.ps1`; all 25 targeted Python tests passed (`tests.test_windowed_dialogs`, `tests.test_xaml_references`, `tests.test_close_window`, `tests.test_project_context_menu`, `tests.test_inspector_footer`); `git diff --check` passed. Server-restart-interrupted checks were rerun. Windows version bumped with `python script/sync_version.py --bump` to 1.0.147 / package 1.0.147.0. The Mac checkout is still absent, so its matching version bump and combined-workspace parity verification cannot be performed here. No menus changed. Not run for this follow-up: full Python discovery (unrelated failures recorded under 1.0.145), live Gemini, macOS, Windows 10, high-contrast runtime, or signed MSIX installation.

## 2026-10-27 - Remove the second dialog card layer (1.0.146 / 1.0.146.0)

Fixed the remaining raised, rounded inner card inside companion dialog windows. The new native regression check reproduced the initial-open bug before any theme switch: `BackgroundElement` still had an 8 px corner radius, `ThemeShadow`, and 128 px elevation. WinUI can reapply its elevation during content preparation after the existing Loaded/Opened cleanup, which is why theme-switch screenshots alone missed it.

The shared host now sets transparent background/border and zero corners directly on the ContentDialog and its card, clears the card's elevation after opening, and observes only that card's Shadow dependency property so later native content preparation cannot restore the second shadow. The wrapper's clipping corners are also removed. The native window keeps its own Windows-rounded frame and DWM shadow; content cards and control corners are untouched. Native smoke checks now verify transparent control/card backgrounds, zero card border/corners/elevation, and no card shadow on initial opening and in light/dark/system modes.

Validation: the strengthened native test failed against 1.0.145's host with `shadow=True, elevation=128`, then passed with the final fix on ARM64 and x64 emulation. Inspected the resulting light/dark Delete dialog captures: a single continuous surface with no inner rounded outline or shadow. All 24 targeted Python tests pass. `powershell script/build-windows.ps1 -Platform ARM64` passed in Debug and Release, and the final full ARM64 UI smoke suite passed, including New Project media inspection and dynamic dialog content. Windows version bumped using `python script/sync_version.py --bump`; macOS version synchronization remains blocked by the absent Mac checkout. No menus changed. Not run for this follow-up: full Python discovery (the two unrelated issues are recorded under 1.0.145), live Gemini, macOS, Windows 10, high-contrast runtime, or signed MSIX installation.

## 2026-10-26 - Close Window command and light-mode error dialogs (1.0.143 / 1.0.143.0)

**File > Close Window** threw `E_ABORT (0x80004004)` from `Window.Close()` because the command closed the window synchronously while its menu flyout was still dismissing. The generic menu handler caught that exception and opened the **Action could not finish** dialog, which is the gray dialog in the report. `CloseWindowDeferred` now dispatches `Close()` after the menu/keyboard handler returns and falls back to `AppWindow.Destroy()` if WinUI still aborts. The native UI smoke test opens a second editor window, closes it through the same `close-window` command, and waits for its `Closed` event.

The dialog host now scales its minimum window size to the monitor DPI. The previous unscaled 480/240 px minimum could shrink a high-DPI dialog below its XAML content, clipping wrapped error text and lower list rows. Confirmation and error copy is also laid out as explicit wrapped lines so the shared ContentDialog cannot cut off the second line.

The gray error dialog also came from the failed close path: it was created while the owner window was already shutting down, so the dialog surface could not settle. The shared host's opaque Fluent surface and smoke-layer removal remain in place, and the smoke suite now verifies both the delete dialog and a close-button-only **Action could not finish** dialog in light mode (`delete-generated-light.png`, `error-dialog-light.png`). Both are white with no dimming layer and the full error message.

Validation on this Windows ARM64 host: `powershell script/build-windows.ps1 -Platform ARM64 -Configuration Debug`; `powershell script/test-windows-ui.ps1 -Platform ARM64` PASS, including the secondary-window Close Window command and the wrapped error dialog; `python -m unittest tests.test_close_window tests.test_project_context_menu tests.test_inspector_footer tests.test_windowed_dialogs tests.test_xaml_references`; `python script/check_xaml_references.py`; `python script/sync_version.py --status` reports 1.0.143 / 1.0.143.0. The editor header no longer duplicates the inspector's project controls: the **Open folder** and inspector visibility buttons were removed from the top-right header. Project actions live in the inspector footer again: **Open project folder**, **Open project configuration**, and the restored **Settings** gear at the bottom-right. Inspector visibility remains available from **View > Show / hide inspector** and **Ctrl+Shift+B**.

Project right-click actions now affect only the clicked project. The delete-generated dialog therefore renders white in light mode instead of gray. Its **Delete selected** action stays disabled until something is checked, switches to the accent state when a selection is made, shows all seven content options, and the native smoke test verifies the visual primary button and every option.

## 2026-10-26 - Windowed dialogs use the app surface with no floating card or border (1.0.137 / 1.0.137.0)

Every Vidi Studio dialog that opens in its own window now shares the app's normal window chrome. The shared `WindowedDialog` host in `Views/WindowAppearance.cs` flattens the WinUI content-dialog template instead of presenting a white bordered card over a dimmed smoke layer: the smoke fill, card background, card border, title-area overlay, and separator line are transparent, so the dialog title, content, and buttons sit directly on the same Mica-backed Fluent surface as the main window and Settings. High contrast and systems without a material still fall back to the opaque Fluent base surface through `WindowAppearance`.

The host now extends the app surface into the caption and supplies a 32-pixel drag region, so the system minimize/maximize/close chrome is transparent over the material and the dialog title appears once inside the content rather than twice (caption plus card). Dialog windows are fixed-size like native dialogs and show only the system Close button. `FitWindowToCard` now always sizes the window to its content, including clipped non-scroll content such as the delete-generated checkbox list, while preserving explicit caps used by wide tables and the export editor; it locates the template's named `BackgroundElement` card so a dialog with its own `Width` is not fitted to the outer container. Large widths now grow past the 548-pixel WinUI default up to the width each caller already requested, so the segment editor's 580-pixel content is no longer clipped.

This covers the delete-generated dialog, confirm/error prompts, rename, export information, project-file issues, narration voice/failure reports, the segment editor, New Project, Export, both Piper voice dialogs, and the Gemini key-deletion confirmation because they all call `WindowedDialog.ShowAsync`. The obsolete `fitToContent` switch was removed from the host and all call sites.

The per-project **Narration during playback** and **Follow synchronized transcript** toggles no longer live in `%APPDATA%\VidiStudio\config.json`. ## 2026-10-26 - Idle gallery stays stable and Piper summary sentences start without preprocessing gaps (1.0.132 / 1.0.132.0)

The All Projects grid/list no longer rebuilds while the app is left open. The four-second integrity timer called `Model.Reload()` whenever the gallery was visible, and `Reload()` cleared and re-added every `StudioProject`, recreating cards, thumbnails, and selection every tick. `StudioViewModel` now caches project instances by folder and reuses them while `project.json` is unchanged, applies filtered results as an incremental list diff, and exposes `ReloadIfProjectsChanged()` so the timer only reloads when a folder or manifest actually changes. `StudioProject` now implements `INotifyPropertyChanged` for integrity fields, letting badge updates reach existing rows without rebuilding them. The engine's atomic project save also retries a transient Windows `PermissionError` (a concurrent reader) instead of failing a completed change.

Piper summary playback no longer launches a fresh speech host and Piper process for every sentence. `SpeechHost` now accepts a `synthesize-batch` request, runs Piper once with `--json-input` so the voice model stays loaded, and writes a `.done` marker per finished WAV; `SummaryReadback` plans the remaining sentences, starts the batch before the first sentence plays, waits only for each sentence's marker, and keeps the playback state at **Playing** between sentences instead of flashing **Preparing**. Rendered sentences are cached (up to 400 WAVs) so replaying a summary is instant, and stale readback folders are cleaned up. Windows OneCore and SAPI 5 paths are unchanged.

Validation on this Windows 11 ARM64 host: `script\build-windows.ps1 -Platform ARM64 -Configuration Debug` and `-Platform x64 -Configuration Debug` both passed and rebuilt the frozen engine; the ARM64 and x64 speech hosts (plus the x86 helper) were republished with the batch command. The ARM64 and x64 (emulated) native UI smoke tests passed all gallery, theme, summary playback/highlighting, editing, OneCore/SAPI narration, export, and engine checks, including a new assertion that repeated gallery reloads keep the same project instance. A direct bundled-Piper check rendered three summary sentences in 2.99 s with one process versus 3.53 s for three separate single-sentence processes, confirming the model is loaded once. `.venv-windows\Scripts\python.exe -m unittest discover -s tests` ran 183 tests with 3 skips and passed when `VIDISTUDIO_SPEECH_HOST`, `VIDISTUDIO_PIPER_PATH`, and `VIDISTUDIO_PIPER_VOICES` were set (without the speech host, the existing system-speech pipeline test has no cue audio). Added `tests/test_gallery_refresh.py`, a transient-lock retry test, and batch-routing source checks. `script\check_menu_parity.py` and `tests.test_menu_parity` skipped because the macOS checkout is absent. Not run: live Gemini, listening checks, signed package installation, and macOS validation. The macOS checkout is absent, so this bump advanced Windows only; the same 1.0.132 version must be mirrored to macOS when that repository is available.

## 2026-10-25 - Fluent material window chrome, extended Mica title bar, and theme-aware captions (1.0.131 / 1.0.131.0)

Windows now uses the system material consistently instead of painting over it. A shared `MaterialBackdrop` controller selects Mica on Windows 11 and Desktop Acrylic on Windows 10, follows the app's light/dark preview through `SystemBackdropConfiguration.Theme`, and falls back to an opaque Fluent base in high contrast or when the composition backdrop is unavailable. The previous hard-coded title-bar colors and opaque `ApplicationPageBackgroundThemeBrush` surfaces were replaced with a translucent `LayerFillColorDefault` layer.

The main window now extends its content into the title bar. A 32-pixel `AppTitleBar` row shows the app icon and **Vidi Studio**, the menu bar sits directly below on the material, and the standard minimize/maximize/close buttons are transparent over Mica (with a one-step alpha so DWM still draws the glyphs). The Settings window uses the same extended title-bar treatment; content dialogs keep their own standard caption but now receive a theme-matched caption color and material client surface. Light and dark full-window captures on this Windows 11 ARM64 host confirmed the Mica gradient across the title bar and menu strip, visible caption buttons, the Settings extended title bar, and the New Project dialog caption.

The XAML-free `MaterialBackdrop` is required because `Application.RequestedTheme` cannot be changed after startup in this host (it throws `COMException`), so the built-in XAML Mica/Acrylic backdrops would stay in the system theme. The custom controller updates its configuration when the preview changes. `WindowAppearance` also handles high contrast, caption foreground/hover/pressed colors, and DWM's immersive-dark caption attribute where available.

Two test-harness defects that blocked the native UI smoke test were corrected: the project-browser assertion expected **New Project** to be disabled without a Gemini key even though its dialog owns that gate (matching the Mac menu), and rebuilding Settings → Narration reused a button that was still parented in the previous visual tree. The delete-voices button is now recreated with its row.

Validation on this Windows 11 ARM64 host: `script\build-windows.ps1 -Platform ARM64 -Configuration Debug` and `-Platform x64 -Configuration Debug` completed with exit code 0; the x64 speech host republish warned because the machine's x86 `dotnet.exe` has no SDK, so the existing x64 helper was used. The ARM64 native UI smoke test passed the new material, extended-title-bar, transparency, theme, gallery, editor, Settings, dialog, export, and menu checks, then stopped at its existing Windows Core narration step because `SpeechHost` exited with code -1 (the same environment speech failure recorded in earlier entries); the remaining smoke assertions were not run. `.venv-windows\Scripts\python.exe -m unittest discover -s tests` ran 177 tests with 4 skips and the known `test_engine.Validation.test_pipeline` system-speech failure (no cue audio); `script\check_xaml_references.py` passed. `script\check_menu_parity.py --allow-missing` and `tests.test_menu_parity` skipped because the macOS checkout is not present. Not run: Windows 10 runtime Acrylic verification, live Gemini, and macOS validation. The macOS checkout is absent, so this bump advanced Windows only; the same 1.0.131 version must be mirrored to macOS when that repository is available.

## 2026-10-25 - Working native context menu, C++ build fixes, and modern-menu validation (1.0.130 / 1.0.130.0)

After the **Desktop development with C++** workload was installed, the native extension was compiled by the real v145/SDK 28000 toolset. `ShellExecuteW` now includes `<shellapi.h>`; the duplicate `WIN32_LEAN_AND_MEAN` and `NOMINMAX` definitions were removed because the project already defines them; and `DllGetClassObject`/`DllCanUnloadNow` use the SDK's `STDAPI` signatures with exports supplied by a new `VidiStudio.ShellExtension.def` module-definition file. Putting `__declspec(dllexport)` on those definitions makes MSVC 14.51 report C2375 (different linkage) against the SDK 28000 declaration, so the `.def` file is the supported export path.

The app builds the extension through a `BuildOptionalShellExtension` MSBuild target with explicit `Configuration`/`Platform` properties and without `CurrentSolutionConfigurationContents`, instead of a project reference. A referenced project excluded from the solution otherwise falls back to the Win32 platform under solution builds. `script\build-windows.ps1` passes `VCTargetsPath` with the trailing backslash the VC targets require (MSBuild consumes one backslash at the command line, so the script passes two). The solution keeps the native project visible with `<Build Project="false" />`, and the target still builds it only when the matching C++ toolset is installed; without the workload, the app builds and runs with the legacy menu.

Validation on this Windows 11 ARM64 host: `script\build-windows.ps1 -Platform ARM64 -Configuration Debug -Rebuild` built `runtime\shell\ARM64\VidiStudio.ShellExtension.dll` (ARM64, with `DllGetClassObject` and `DllCanUnloadNow` exported) and the app with exit code 0; `MSBuild "Vidi Studio Windows.slnx" /t:Rebuild` also passed with the native project built through the target, and the same build with `VidiShellExtensionBuild=false` completed without the DLL. `-Package` produced `Vidi Studio Windows_1.0.130.0_arm64_Debug.msix`, and `script\verify-windows-package.py --platform arm64` passed including the shell-extension architecture and COM/context-menu registrations. The package was registered, and the Windows 11 modern File Explorer menu showed **Describe with Vidi Studio** for a test `.mp4`; invoking it launched the app with `--video "C:\...\menu-test.mp4"` as recorded in `diagnostics.log` and opened the project. The test project and video were deleted afterward. The Windows Python suite ran 177 tests with 4 skips and one unrelated environment failure in `test_engine.Validation.test_pipeline` (system speech produced no cue audio), which does not exercise the changed files. Not run: live Gemini and macOS validation. The macOS checkout was not present, so this bump advanced the Windows version only; the same patch version must be mirrored to macOS when that repository is available.

## 2026-10-25 - Optional native context menu, clean build on VS 2026, and Storage label (1.0.129 / 1.0.129.0)

Visual Studio 2026 builds failed with MSB8020 because the machine has the WinUI/.NET and Windows SDK workloads but not the **Desktop development with C++** workload, so the pinned/looked-up v143 toolset (and the ARM64 desktop C++ tools) did not exist. The native `VidiStudio.ShellExtension` project was an unconditional project reference and its DLL an unconditional content item, so the whole app build stopped and the copy failed with MSB3030.

The shell extension is now optional. `Vidi Studio Windows.csproj` detects the installed VC targets through `VCTargetsPath`, `VSInstallRoot`, or `MSBuildExtensionsPath32` and enables the extension only when a v145/v144/v143/v142 platform toolset exists for the selected architecture. The project reference and packaged DLL content are conditional, and the build prints one informational note when the toolset is absent. `Vidi Studio Windows.slnx` keeps the extension project visible but marks it `<Build Project="false" />`, so a solution build does not build it directly; when the C++ workload is installed, the app project reference still builds it. The legacy `Show more options` / registry menu continues to work without the DLL. Installing the C++ workload (with the ARM64 or x64 build tools) and rebuilding restores the Windows 11 modern menu.

The compiler warning CS0649 in `PiperDeleteVoicesDialog.cs` is fixed by removing the unused `deleting` field; the existing `deletingVoiceId` state already covers the same guards. The Settings sidebar item formerly labelled **Storage and tools** is now **Storage**, and the related Help text, New Project error text, engine FFmpeg message, README, and feature-parity wording were updated to match (macOS keeps its own label).

The taskbar showed the generic Windows app placeholder even though the title bar and executable used `AppIcon.ico`. The package logo PNGs (`Square44x44Logo`, `Square150x150Logo`, `Wide310x150Logo`, `StoreLogo`, splash, and lock-screen assets) were project content without `CopyToOutputDirectory`, so the loose layout registered next to `AppxManifest.xml` did not contain the files the manifest references. All asset variants now copy to the output directory, which restores the Vidi Studio icon in the taskbar, Start menu, and window previews.

Validation on this Windows 11 ARM64 host: `script\build-windows.ps1 -Platform ARM64 -Configuration Debug` completed with exit code 0, and `MSBuild "Vidi Studio Windows.slnx" /t:Build` completed with only the pre-existing PRI249 resource-qualifier warnings. The loose ARM64 package layout was registered and launched; `diagnostics.log` recorded `App constructed - version 1.0.129.0`, `Main window created.`, and `Launch completed.` with the process responding, and a screen capture confirmed the Vidi Studio camera icon in the taskbar instead of the generic placeholder. The Windows Python suite ran 177 tests with 4 skips and one unrelated environment failure in `test_engine.Validation.test_pipeline` (system speech produced no cue audio), which does not exercise the changed files. Not run here: the native C++ extension (the C++ workload is absent), MSIX packaging with the extension DLL, live Gemini, and macOS validation. The macOS checkout was not present, so this bump advanced the Windows version only; the same patch version must be mirrored to macOS when that repository is available.

## 2026-10-25 — Version sync and Visual Studio toolset compatibility (1.0.128 / 1.0.128.0)

The desktop version was advanced in lockstep for the macOS Finder Sync **Describe with Vidi Studio** context-menu extension and Settings enablement controls. The Windows modern context-menu integration from 1.0.127 is unchanged.

The native shell-extension project no longer pins Platform Toolset v143 and no longer overwrites an existing toolset with an empty value. It preserves a valid toolset selected by Visual Studio, otherwise falls back through the installed **v145/v144/v143/v142** PlatformToolsets, and detects v170/v180 VC targets. `script/build-windows.ps1` discovers the VC targets beside the selected MSBuild installation and passes `VCTargetsPath`, covering command-line builds as well. The README now lists the **Desktop development with C++** workload as a build requirement. This resolves the Visual Studio errors that v143 could not be found and that the PlatformToolset property was not defined.

Validation: on this macOS host the Windows Python suite passed 200 tests (1 Windows-only skip), the macOS suite passed 155 tests, and menu parity passed. macOS `script/build_and_run.sh --verify` built and launched 1.0.128 with 13 Help pages, and the Finder context menu was verified in Finder. Not run on this host: native Windows/MSBuild compilation, MSIX packaging, and Windows UI checks of the toolset change.

## 2026-10-25 — Modern Windows context menu and developer-only repair action (1.0.127 / 1.0.127.0)

The Windows 11 modern File Explorer context menu now includes **Describe with Vidi Studio** directly. A new native `ShellExtension` project implements `IExplorerCommand`, exports the COM class factory, uses the app icon, and launches the packaged app through `IApplicationActivationManager` with `--video` and the selected path. `Package.appxmanifest` registers the DLL with `windows.comServer` and `windows.fileExplorerContextMenus` (`desktop4`/`desktop5`), while the existing HKCU verbs continue to serve the legacy **Show more options** menu. The Storage action is now **Repair legacy File Explorer context menu**, appears only with Developer mode, and is grouped with the advanced executable-path overrides.

## 2026-10-25 — Advanced executable paths gated by Developer mode (1.0.126 / 1.0.126.0)

Developer mode now controls the remaining advanced executable settings. With it off, **Storage and tools** shows the project library and the engine/diagnostics actions but not the Python, FFmpeg, or FFprobe executable fields. **Narration** hides the optional SAPI host executable even when Windows SAPI 5 is selected. Enabling Developer mode reveals all four controls; disabling it hides them again without clearing saved paths. The controls remain available to speech and media execution while hidden.

## 2026-10-25 — Inspector issue-list dialog (1.0.123 / 1.0.123.0)

Windows now mirrors the Mac inspector affordance: when a project has more than one file-integrity issue, a warning icon appears at the right edge of the **Project Information** header in the inspector. Its tooltip and UI Automation name report the exact issue count, and its color is red for damaged projects or orange for recoverable warnings. ## 2026-10-25 — Project file integrity and damaged-project reporting (1.0.122 / 1.0.122.0)

Windows mirrors the Mac project audit. `Models/ProjectIntegrity.cs` checks project.json, the original local video, metadata/probe.json, referenced timeline pictures, segment narration audio, and exports, and classifies the result as healthy, warning, or damaged. One recoverable category (for example all narration files) stays a warning; missing project.json, a missing local source, or loss of at least 75% of tracked files across multiple generated categories is damaged. `StudioViewModel` audits on every reload, keeps missing folders as damaged placeholders for opened projects, and upgrades placeholders when project.json is restored. The editor gains a second `InfoBar` below the Gemini key bar with the exact missing file names, impact, regeneration/re-export actions, and a Delete Project button for damaged projects; a details dialog lists every missing path. Gallery cards and the list view show Damaged or Files Missing badges, context-menu analysis/export/rename actions are gated on usable files, and the Recycle Bin confirmation distinguishes damaged projects from ordinary deletions. The project-browser context-menu regression test now asserts the file-integrity gate.

Validation: on this macOS host the Windows Python suite passed 199 tests (1 Windows-only skip); the Mac suite passed 151 tests; both `test_menu_parity` suites and `script/check_menu_parity.py` passed; the new Mac project-integrity harness passed; macOS `script/build_and_run.sh --verify` built, signed, and launched 1.0.122 with 13 Help pages. The changed `Models/StudioProject.cs` and new `Models/ProjectIntegrity.cs` compiled cleanly in a temporary net10.0 harness with 0 warnings and 0 errors, and that harness passed healthy, missing-narration warning, missing-source damaged, corrupt/missing-manifest placeholder, missing-folder, and path-traversal cases. ## 2026-10-25 — Summary sentence and paragraph navigation (1.0.119 / 1.0.119.0)

The Summary page now centers Previous/Next Sentence and Previous/Next Paragraph buttons around Play/Pause and Stop. `SummaryReadback` parses the rendered summary into sentences grouped by Markdown-style paragraph blocks, tracks the active sentence index, and exposes boundary-aware navigation; every navigation call cancels current playback on the existing playback gate and reads the selected sentence through the same OneCore, SAPI 5, Piper, or Gemini path as continuous reading. `MainWindow` syncs the button and menu enabled states from the reader, resets the highlight search anchor when jumping, and rebuilds the reader source whenever the summary is rendered. Playback → Summary Reading now includes the four navigation commands with `Ctrl+Shift+[` / `]` for sentences and `Ctrl+Alt+[` / `]` for paragraphs. ## 2026-10-24 — Summary playback stops on project or video changes (1.0.118 / 1.0.118.0)

Summary read-aloud now stops automatically when the project changes or the video starts playing. `ProjectReloaded` — raised after every engine command reloads the project, including transcript and description saves, summary edits, analysis, and narration — calls `readback.Stop()` before the refreshed view appears, and the playback timer stops the reader on the transition into playback for both the local video and the YouTube player. macOS applies the same rule through `project.updatedAt` and `playback.playing`, so both desktops behave identically. Manual Play, Pause, and Stop are unchanged.

## 2026-10-24 — Piper summary playback, live timing, and voice management (1.0.114 / 1.0.114.0)

Windows summary read-aloud with Piper used to fail immediately because each sentence ran through a second engine process; Piper and SAPI 5 now synthesize through the same speech host as live narration, so the summary plays and any failure carries its own message. Symbol-only summary lines (horizontal rules, bullets) are skipped instead of reaching the speech engine. Live narration pre-renders upcoming Piper cues into a small cache (`live-ready-*.wav`, capped at 8 files) so a cue begins at its first word instead of partway through; the playback timer warms the next cue within 20 seconds. The Narration settings page drops the long Piper descriptions and adds Delete voices beside Download or remove voices. A new `PiperDeleteVoicesDialog` lists only downloaded voices with Play Sample, the same search, language/quality filters, and four sort orders as the catalog, and a red Delete; Escape closes both Piper dialogs.

The macOS engine now hands Piper a short espeak-ng data path because espeak-ng truncates paths at 160 bytes, which made Piper speech fail only inside the signed app bundle (Windows paths are short and its speech host is unaffected). macOS summary read-aloud labels the selected engine (**Piper Speech** or **Apple Speech**) and shows Piper's own guidance instead of the Apple-only message.

## 2026-10-24 — Piper download and removal progress inside the voice dialog (1.0.111 / 1.0.111.0)

`PiperVoicesDialog` now shows transfer progress inside the affected voice row. Each row owns a determinate progress bar plus a percentage and size line (`67% · 42.3 MB of 63.1 MB`) that are updated in place by the download reporter, so the 178-row list is not rebuilt on every tick. While a voice downloads, its action button becomes a disabled **Downloading…** state with a spinner, every other row's actions are disabled, and the dialog-level progress bar and status line mirror the same percentage; search and filters stay disabled until the transfer finishes. Delete is now asynchronous and reports its own progress: the row shows an indeterminate **Deleting…** state with a spinner, all actions are disabled, the dialog bar switches to indeterminate, `PiperService.DeleteVoice` runs off the UI thread, and the row returns to **Download** once the model, configuration, and cached sample are removed. UI Automation names were added for the catalog list, the aggregate bar, each row's bar and ring, and the downloading/deleting action states. README, architecture, acceptance, website docs, and Mac Help describe both indicators. Version bumped to 1.0.111.

Validation: on this macOS host, the Windows Python suite (`../VidiStudio Mac/.venv/bin/python -m unittest discover -s tests`) passes 196 tests (1 Windows-only skip), including 13 `test_piper` tests covering the row progress controls, in-place reporting, deletion task, and automation names. ## 2026-10-24 — Piper voice browser: filters, sorting, readable languages, and sample preview (1.0.110 / 1.0.110.0)

The Piper download dialog now works like a voice browser instead of a single search box. `PiperCatalogVoice` carries the catalog's language family, English language name, and English country, and renders a plain-English label such as **English (United States)** rather than `en-US`; quality values map to readable labels (`x_low` → Extra low) with a rank used for ordering. `PiperVoicesDialog` adds Language and Quality dropdowns (each with an "All …" entry), a Sort by dropdown (Language A–Z, Voice name A–Z, Quality high to low, Size smallest first), keeps the search box as an additional filter, and reports how many of the 178 voices are shown and how many are downloaded. Every row keeps its Download/Delete button and gains a **Play sample** button: it downloads the short published `samples/speaker_0.mp3` beside the model on first use into `%APPDATA%\VidiStudio\piper\samples`, caches it for instant replay, plays it through `MediaPlayer` (speech audio category) with a 30-second load timeout and Stop support, and needs the internet only for that preview. `PiperService.DeleteVoice` now removes the cached sample too, and the settings dialog opens the taller dialog. Because the app was reported not to open after the previous build, `App.OnLaunched` now appends startup milestones (app construction, activation kind and arguments, main window created, launch completed, single-instance redirect, and startup failure with the exception) to `%APPDATA%\VidiStudio\diagnostics.log`, so a silent launch failure can be diagnosed even when no window appears.

Validation: on this macOS host, the Windows Python suite (`../VidiStudio Mac/.venv/bin/python -m unittest discover -s tests`) passes 195 tests (1 Windows-only skip), including `test_piper` coverage for English language/dialect fields, sample paths, filters, sort orders, sample caching, and cache deletion, plus `test_file_explorer_activation` coverage for the startup log. Menu parity checker and both `test_menu_parity` suites pass; website `npm run lint` and `npm run build` pass; macOS packaged `script/build_and_run.sh --verify` built and launched 1.0.110 with 13 Help pages. Not run: native Windows build/MSIX packaging, C# compilation of the new `MediaPlayer`/record code, actual sample playback or Piper synthesis on Windows, and UI/Narrator smoke tests.

## 2026-10-24 — Bundled Piper voices (1.0.109 / 1.0.109.0)

Windows now offers **Piper Voices** as a fourth speech engine next to Windows OneCore, Windows SAPI 5, and Gemini Voices. The official Rhasspy Piper Windows x64 runtime (`2023.11.14-2`, SHA-256 `f3c58906402b24f3a96d92145f58acba6d86c9b5db896d207f78dc80811efcea`) is fetched at build time by `script/fetch-piper.ps1`, verified, and packaged under `piper/`. A packaged catalog of 178 voices (`Assets/piper-voices.json`) powers a searchable download dialog (`PiperVoicesDialog`); only selected `.onnx` and `.onnx.json` files are downloaded to `%APPDATA%\VidiStudio\piper\voices`, with size and MD5 verification and delete support. `SpeechHost` runs the bundled `piper.exe` with `--model`, `--config`, `--output_file`, `--length_scale` (inverse rate), optional speaker, and text on stdin. `SpeechService`, `OnDeviceNarrator`, `SummaryReadback`, the Python engine, settings preview, and generation all route Piper through the same path. Summary read-aloud uses the narration voice. Docs, Help, README, architecture/acceptance/parity pages, and the website were updated.

Validation: on this macOS host, the Windows Python suite (`../VidiStudio Mac/.venv/bin/python -m unittest discover -s tests`) passes 189 tests (1 Windows-only skip), including `test_piper`, `test_windows_speech`, and `test_narration_playback_options`; the menu parity checker and `test_menu_parity` pass. macOS packaged `script/build_and_run.sh --verify` passed (13 Help pages); the Mac suite passed 145 tests; website `npm run lint` and `npm run build` pass. Not run: native Windows build/MSIX packaging, actual Piper synthesis on Windows, UI smoke tests, and signed-package installation. Versions synchronized at 1.0.109 / Windows package 1.0.109.0.

## 2026-10-19 — File > New Project disabled without a Gemini API key (1.0.100 / 1.0.100.0)

The Windows File > New Project… command is now disabled when no Gemini API key is present. `MainWindow.Menus.cs` `CanRunDesktopCommand("new-project")` returns `Model.HasApiKey` (still idle-gated) instead of always true, matching the already-disabled gallery "New project" button and the Mac command; the command handler still redirects to Settings → Gemini API if it is invoked. `MainWindow.SmokeTest.cs` now asserts the New Project command is disabled in the no-key menu state. The macOS app gates the same command in the same task.

## 2026-10-19 — Escape cancels hosted dialogs on Windows (1.0.97 / 1.0.97.0)

Windows dialog windows now cancel on Escape. `WindowedDialog` adds an Escape `KeyboardAccelerator` scoped to both the host window and the `ContentDialog`, guarded by the dialog's `Opened`/`Closed` state so it can never double-fire, and hides the dialog with a `None` result — the same outcome as the Close/Cancel button or the title-bar close. This covers New Project and every other dialog shown through the shared host. macOS already dismisses the New Project sheet with Escape via the Cancel button's `.keyboardShortcut(.cancelAction)`, so no macOS change was needed.

Validation: `script/check_xaml_references.py` passed; Windows `test_parity` (9) passed; version synchronized at 1.0.97 (Mac build 97 / Windows package 1.0.97.0). Native Windows key-handling was not run on this macOS host.

## 2026-10-19 — Larger New Project dialog with a correct card measurement (1.0.96 / 1.0.96.0)

The previous fit collapsed the New Project window onto an inner frame border and made the dialog smaller than its content, clipping the project-type options and adding a horizontal scrollbar. `WindowAppearance.FindCard` now walks the ancestors and selects the largest border that does not fill the whole host — the real dialog card, not the full-size smoke layer above or an inner frame below — and caches it so later layout passes cannot drift. The dialog grew to a 560-wide card (`ContentDialogMaxWidth`) with content widened to 490 (rows/cards 460, YouTube URL 290) and a 470 minimum content height, so the source picker, file row, video preview, and YouTube fields all fit without scrolling; the host opens at 700×820 and then fits the card. All controls are visible without scrolling, and the window still has no minimize or maximize.

Validation: `script/check_xaml_references.py` passed; Windows `test_parity` (9) and `test_menu_parity` (10) passed; version synchronized at 1.0.96 (Mac build 96 / Windows package 1.0.96.0). Native Windows rendering was not run on this macOS host.

Windows mirrors the Mac existing-asset workflow and shared engine. Per-segment **Import narration audio…** is in the segment More Options/context menu with fit validation and replacement confirmation. Segment rows expose the six provenance labels and an amber overlap badge driven by the persisted timing audit; the summary editor gained a Markdown toolbar, provenance picker, shortcuts, list continuation, and preview dialog.

The model carries imported assets, summary origin, timing audit, import reports, and narration source metadata; AD Overview reports narration sources, imported files, mixed-source warnings, and imported-audio labels without treating them as a different generated voice. The Export dialog offers **Audio Description source** when generated and imported audio both exist and routes closed/open/audio-only described exports through combine-imported-audio when imported audio is selected; closed described video remains the default and open remains an explicit single mix.

## 2026-11-01 — Choose audio after YouTube export retrieval fails (1.0.196)

Added a native recovery dialog on Mac and Windows when YouTube soundtrack retrieval fails during original-audio export, described-audio export, or YouTube Import & Combine. Choose Audio File… uses the existing inspect/import path, including unreadable-file rejection, duration checking, explicit mismatch/unknown-duration confirmation, and replacement confirmation. An accepted file becomes the portable export soundtrack and retries the same command with the original format, narration source, and export-replacement choice. Playback and transcription preferences remain independent. Cancel in the failure dialog, file picker, or timing confirmation leaves the export stopped. Export cancellation and mixing/encoding failures do not offer download recovery.

The shared soundtrack resolver marks retrieval-entry failures with an explicit recovery code; exceptions after the soundtrack is yielded retain normal error handling. Native recovery waits for the failed operation to finish and clear its busy state before importing/retrying. The Windows engine client carries the recovery code through ProcessingException into the view model and owner window. Recovery retains the originating project rather than applying to an unrelated project. Canonical Help export/troubleshooting guidance, generated Windows Help, website export/recovery guide, and website FAQ now describe the flow.

Checks: Mac regression suite passes 251 tests; Windows repository suite passes 344 tests on this Mac with one platform-only skip. Five focused tests cover retrieval failure, worker-start failure, post-download mixing failure, cancellation, and imported-source bypass. A real temporary media fixture verifies that a failed download preserves a prior MP3 export, then importing a matching original completes the same MP3 replacement without another download and records the filename/source. Shared export/source parity passes (53 export/progress functions); menu parity remains aligned. The changed Windows engine client, ProcessingException, view model, audio-source controller, models, and source dialogs compile against cached WinUI/Windows SDK references with unrelated UI/speech dependencies supplied by an isolated harness. This semantic check is not a native Windows app build or runtime test.

## 2026-11-05 — Independent Gemini output languages (1.0.217 / build 217)

Settings > Gemini API now has Transcript, Summary, and Descriptions sections, each with an independent Output language selector. Auto (detect from video) is the default. Auto transcription preserves all spoken languages and original scripts; Auto summaries/descriptions use the primary language detected from the video itself, with English when no language is identifiable. A specific choice translates dialogue or generates prose in that language. The shared bundled catalogue has 110 language/script choices, including separate Simplified/Traditional Chinese, based on Google's documented Gemini text-language support checked on 2026-11-05 (https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/models/google-models#language_support). The Models API does not advertise per-model language lists; ASR input-language hints are not translation-output settings. Both desktop controls and engine validation use the same catalogue in ai-defaults.json. Preferences save immediately and remain independent of model choices.

Language instructions reach local/YouTube full generation, imported-audio transcription, transcript/description range re-analysis, and description rewrites. Explicit choices take precedence over original-language instructions in task and Multilingual Media prompts while retaining JSON structure, source speech timing, speaker identities, and existing validation. With a dedicated speech model selected, explicitly translated transcripts use the saved YouTube video model for local media and YouTube, including imported audio; Auto keeps the dedicated speech-model path and its automatic detection. Range editing retains its compatible-model fallback. Auto rewrites preserve the supplied description's language. OCR and TTS remain outside these three output settings. Existing content/imports are unchanged until a requested generation or range action; narration voices are selected separately. Existing English embedded-export language metadata is documented as a current limitation.

User follow-up: removed the entire standalone explanatory block beginning “Language choices use Google’s documented Gemini text-language support” from Settings on both desktops. Its language-support, saving, existing-content, and narration-voice guidance remains in canonical Help and website documentation. The three labelled language controls and their task-specific Auto explanations remain.

## 2026-11-05 — Language-specific saved results and section targets (1.0.221 / build 221)

Implemented portable schema 3 with stable result IDs, Auto as its own category, origin/version/timestamps, source-result and imported-file provenance, persistent review drafts, and selections remembered separately per language/content type. Existing project content migrates idempotently to Auto; selected results remain synchronized with the legacy top-level reader fields. Both desktop engines use the same language-results implementation and command dispatcher. Schema 3 projects should be used with the updated desktop versions so inactive results and their files remain protected.

Project Language is the shared default. Transcript, Summary, and Descriptions/Narration can independently choose Auto, a supported Gemini text language, or Follow Project Language. Keep All Sections in Sync resets overrides without deleting results. The inspector and matching menu/filter panels follow the active tab: transcript controls on Transcript, summary controls on Summary, description/narration controls on Descriptions/AD Overview, and the paired sources on AD Transcript. Target-language changes, project-language changes, synchronization, and Assign Language require Edit mode; assignment relabels existing content without translating it. View mode permits saved-version switching within the current language. Edit is available for empty timed sections to set their first language target. Unsaved summary drafts block language/result switching until saved or cancelled; accepting another-language review never silently changes generation targets.

Generation, imports, segment reanalysis, and narration operate on isolated alternatives. New-language results save directly; existing-language alternatives persist for Keep Both (default), Replace Selected, or Discard New review. Explicitly selected context/result IDs are captured before operations. Regenerate All runs stages without clearing other results, narration, exports, thumbnails, or history. Result-specific edits and histories remain independent. Imports label the supplied text, preserve source assets/combined AD pairing, and support narration alternatives. Batch exports freeze explicitly chosen results per language, validate selections/timing/narration before processing, retain completed outputs on failure/cancellation, and record language/result provenance with distinct names. Captions and narration tracks use language-specific titles/metadata; unknown or mixed Auto is undetermined. Native MOV accessibility finalization preserves the selected language.

Deletion selects individual results, languages, components, narration/history, optional exports, and project-wide thumbnails; exact affected result/export/file counts are previewed before confirmation. Section actions prefill the effective section language/result. Retained results, drafts, histories, and shared imported/audio files are checked before cleanup. Project browsers, saved-result review, segment filters, Created Exports, and matching menus include language/result controls; All Languages is distinct from Auto. Mac/Windows menu labels, hierarchy, guards, and stable commands match. Settings now has one Default Language for New Projects (Auto by default); a common prior three-language preference is preserved, differing preferences migrate to Auto.

## 2026-11-05 — Segment editors require Edit mode (1.0.222 / build 222)

Transcript, Description, and combined AD Transcript row editors now require active Edit mode at every opening path. Mac double-click, row pencil/context Edit, Add Segment, selected-range reanalysis, screen-text insertion, and bulk editing use guarded presentation. The guard runs before pausing/capturing the playhead and again in the asynchronous capture callback, preventing a delayed callback from opening a sheet after Edit mode ends. Ending Edit mode clears pending editors; manual save also checks eligibility. Add/reanalysis menu commands and shortcuts are disabled outside Edit mode on both desktops. Windows EditSegment has a central entry guard; row context menus no longer automatically switch into Edit mode. Context playback/narration actions use their own row tags without requiring an editor mode change. Frame text can still be reviewed read-only outside Edit mode; insertion opens an editable draft only in Edit mode. Summary editing behavior is unchanged.

Website lint/TypeScript/build and desktop/mobile documentation browser checks pass (existing bundle-size advisory only).

## View Project Language and AD Overview — 1.0.225 / build 225

View Project Language is the single shared viewing selector on both desktops, available without Edit mode in the inspector and Language and Saved Results dialog. It switches saved transcripts, captions, summaries, descriptions, timeline, and playback together. AD Overview always follows that same selection; the additional Overview Viewing Language dropdown from interim 1.0.224 has been removed. Older overview-specific preferences are ignored by native readers and removed on engine migration. Saved-result pickers use the shared viewing language and preserve per-language choices. Independent generation targets are explicitly labelled Generation Language and remain Edit-only, as does Assign Language. Changing view does not change explicit generation targets; Follow Project Language uses the viewing language for future generation.

Top-level projections and edit histories now belong to the viewed language, while full generation can target an independent language. Segment narration, rewriting, edits, and undo/redo target the viewed result. Imports assign language metadata without changing generation targets. Regenerate All captures each stage's own target. Native Mac/Windows readers reconstruct the viewed projection directly from saved records on reopening, including provenance and histories. Engine migration adds languageViewVersion and reprojects disk-loaded manifests before capture, protecting saved Auto content from older per-section top-level projections. A language without content shows empty sections/zero overview counts. AD Overview uses the selected description result's own narration settings and timing audit; it does not inherit another language's metadata.

## Language-specific export folders — 1.0.226 / build 226

All new export types now use exports/<language code>/ inside the portable project: video, audio, subtitle/text/JSON/Markdown files, and complete Web Playback packages (for example exports/en/, exports/hi/, exports/auto/). Existing language/result identifiers in filenames are retained. Both native filename previews use the chosen export language, including when it differs from the editor viewing language. Export dialogs describe the folder layout.

The next export operation reorganizes existing tracked exports using saved exportResults language metadata; older outputs without language provenance use Auto. A complete web package moves as one directory so all player assets, subtitles, playlists, and media retain their relative links. exports, exportResults, exportMediaInfo, and exportAudioSources references are updated together; project content and internal source/narration assets are not relocated. Migration chooses numbered filenames on conflict, retains the occupied destination (including registering a previously untracked colliding export), and never overwrites it. An atomic move journal supports recovery if interrupted between renaming and manifest persistence; pending moves resume before subsequent project operations/cleanup. Move and retained-file paths are confined to the project exports tree. Repeated organization is idempotent. Invalid batch selections are validated before export-folder migration begins. Direct combined imported-audio exports now keep their new export records instead of restoring the prior export list.

Cleanup now descends through language folders, preserving ancestors of tracked files and every asset inside a retained web package while removing unreferenced temporary exports. Deleting one output/language therefore preserves retained exports in other folders. Existing replacement confirmation remains required for an output being regenerated. Failed/cancelled batches retain completed files and the repaired manifest links.

## 1.0.228 — repair historical narration references; separate audio/segment deletion (2026-11-06)

Fixed the persistent historical-narration warning after current AD audio is regenerated. After narration generation or Project Cleanup, the engine repairs dangling audio links only inside undo/redo snapshots. It shares a usable nonempty recording from a saved description result in the same language only for an exact text/start/end match. Otherwise it removes unavailable narration metadata from that older edit while preserving its text, timing and undo/redo position, so narration can be generated if restored. Each repair retains the unavailable filename, prior voice/settings and repair decision in historyNarrationRepair. Current saved-result cues and review drafts are neither replaced nor approved by this maintenance, and genuinely missing current result files still produce integrity warnings. No missing-file warning is merely hidden.

A subsequent read finds zero missing historical audio references, with audit metadata retained in all affected snapshots.

Delete Content by Language now offers explicit mutually exclusive Description Audio Only (Keep Segments) and Description Segments and Audio options on both desktops. Audio-only preserves description text/timing/history; the second removes the selected description result and its segments/audio. Saved-result lists filter to the chosen content types, Select Visible Results behaves consistently, hidden ineligible result selections are pruned, and confirmations use readable action labels. Shared files referenced by retained results/history remain protected and physical file counts are previewed before deletion. No desktop menu items changed.

## 2026-11-06 — 1.0.230: automatic Windows Settings

Windows Settings now applies and persists application preferences automatically, with no Save settings or Cancel footer buttons. The native window close control/Alt-F4 retains changes. A central observer saves after a brief pause, flushes on deactivation and normal/programmatic close, retries storage failures without accepting a failed baseline, and blocks normal closure while a write is pending. Open editors receive preference updates without closing Settings, including captions, narration context, theme and Developer-mode commands. Project Settings actions continue saving to their selected project.

Only edited preferences and nested model/prompt fields are merged into the latest settings, preserving unrelated changes from other windows/engine operations. Gemini keys are passed separately to Windows Credential Manager and never included in persisted settings JSON. Explicit key deletion uses the same checked persistence path. Native smoke expectations now check automatic persistence while Settings remains open, close-time flushing, editor caption refresh and the absence of footer buttons; these native checks were added but could not be executed here.

## 2026-11-06 — 1.0.233: selected multilingual video and audio tracks

Export → Video/Audio now offers One File with Selected Tracks alongside the existing separate-language export workflow. Each language has one AD Audio result picker (generated descriptions/narration or imported AD audio) and one caption/transcript result picker. AD Transcript can pair descriptions explicitly; when generated AD audio is included, its selected description result also supplies AD Transcript text. No export-all-versions choice exists. Engine validation rejects duplicate languages within either audio or caption tracks and inconsistent generated AD audio/text versions.

Selectable audio/closed captions support several languages in one container. Mixed AD audio and burned captions each allow one language across the entire file; their choices are independent. Local video supports MKV/MP4/MOV/M4V; multi-track audio supports M4A/MKA, including the existing YouTube soundtrack source path. WAV/MP3 remain separate single-soundtrack exports. Original Audio is optionally retained as the default selectable audio track. AD audio is a described program mix, not a translation/dub of source dialogue. Player/default caption behavior varies by container; MKV/MKA provide the clearest track/accessibility metadata.

Track plans freeze explicit saved-result IDs, paired captions, presentation and formats at Save. All jobs validate before processing; validation checks current narration, readable recordings, segment duration, selected transcript/description timing and one result per language. Temporary audio/caption sources and incomplete output are removed on failure/cancellation; existing destinations commit only after validation. Auto remains Auto in filenames/titles, with undetermined container language metadata. New filenames use readable language names and stable selection identifiers. Multi-language files live under combined folders such as exports/en+hi/ and exports/auto+en/; language lists and selected-result provenance survive organization and match Created Exports/deletion filters. MKA is classified as audio on both desktops. Separate-language exports now use readable language names too.

Fixed an existing replacement-provenance defect discovered during this work: a replace-enabled batch job could relabel unrelated tracked exports. Registration now updates only newly created paths or the specifically committed replacement. Regression coverage verifies another multilingual output and its provenance remain unchanged.
